Whenever it happens, my stomach just turns.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have you been going out of your way to avoid reminders of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still have to go to work every day and do my job. So I'm still right there in the warehouse, around the pallets and the forklifts. I haven't really been trying to stay away from any of it, even though it bothers me when I'm there.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have you been feeling unusually jumpy or easily startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't noticed myself jumping at things or being more startled than usual. Like I said, the noises at work bother me and bring the memories back, but I don't physically jump or feel on edge like that. So, no, I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have you been trying not to think about it or push the feelings about it away?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I do that. Um, I mean, I don't really try to block it out or anything. When it pops into my head, it just happens. I'm not actively trying to push the thoughts or the feelings away. I just kind of deal with it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have you felt detached from yourself, like you were outside yourself or unreal?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt anything like that. Um, I feel like myself. I haven't had any moments where I felt like I was outside my body or like things weren't real. I just feel normal in that way, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. I mean, I'm actually not able to do my regular receiving work right now. Um... I was doing a pallet move recently and I just froze up, and then I ended up snapping at someone. My supervisor had to take me off the floor after that because I just wasn't functioning safely out there. So, yeah, it's definitely making things hard at work.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start right away after the accident?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's been going on for about three weeks now. It all started right after that day when the pallet collapsed and my coworker got hurt. So, yeah, ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have your reactions felt stronger than you would have expected for what happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I've just been... snapping at people over really small stuff. Like, the other day, one of the seasonal guys set some boxes down just a little crooked, and I just went off. I hear myself getting sharp and loud before I can even pull it back. It's just... I don't normally react like that, but lately, it's like I have no patience at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

My supervisor had to take me off the floor after that because I just wasn't functioning safely out there. So, yeah, it's definitely making things hard at work.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start right away after the accident?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's been going on for about three weeks now. It all started right after that day when the pallet collapsed and my coworker got hurt. So, yeah, ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have your reactions felt stronger than you would have expected for what happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I've just been... snapping at people over really small stuff. Like, the other day, one of the seasonal guys set some boxes down just a little crooked, and I just went off. I hear myself getting sharp and loud before I can even pull it back. It's just... I don't normally react like that, but lately, it's like I have no patience at all.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than one month since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, not quite a month yet. Like I mentioned a little bit ago, it's been about three weeks. Just... ever since that day the pallet collapsed and injured my coworker.\n\nDoctor: Have these reactions been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. My supervisor had to step in and actually take me off the floor. He told me I just wasn't functioning safely. I hear myself getting sharp and loud before I can even pull it back. It's just... I don't normally react like that, but lately, it's like I have no patience at all.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than one month since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, not quite a month yet. Like I mentioned a little bit ago, it's been about three weeks. Just... ever since that day the pallet collapsed and injured my coworker.\n\nDoctor: Have these reactions been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I just kind of deal with it when it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have you felt detached from yourself, like you were outside yourself or unreal?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't felt anything like that. Um, I feel like myself. I haven't had any moments where I felt like I was outside my body or like things weren't real. I just feel normal in that way, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, has this been making it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. I mean, I'm actually not able to do my regular receiving work right now. Um... I was doing a pallet move recently and I just froze up, and then I ended up snapping at someone. My supervisor had to take me off the floor after that because I just wasn't functioning safely out there. So, yeah, it's definitely making things hard at work.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start right away after the accident?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's been going on for about three weeks now. It all started right after that day when the pallet collapsed and my coworker got hurt. So, yeah, ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have your reactions felt stronger than you would have expected for what happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I've just been... snapping at people over really small stuff. Like, the other day, one of the seasonal guys set some boxes down just a little crooked, and I just went off. I hear myself getting sharp and loud before I can even pull it back. It's just... I don't normally react like that, but lately, it's like I have no patience at all.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than one month since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, not quite a month yet. Like I mentioned a little bit ago, it's been about three weeks. Just... ever since that day the pallet collapsed and injured my coworker.\n\nDoctor: Have these reactions been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. My supervisor had to step in and actually take me off the floor. He told me I just wasn't functioning safely. So, yeah, it's definitely causing a lot of problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Since the accident, have there been times when you felt emotionally numb, like you couldn’t feel love, happiness, or closeness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... actually, that's kind of what made me finally come in here.
